Capability run — manager report

End-to-end exercise of discovery, metadata, and pattern-scan SAST across all five supported artifact types (APK, IPA, JAR, native .so, SDK directories). Results are leads, not confirmed vulnerabilities. This page includes per-finding severity_score, taxonomy, cvss_estimate, How to fix, and How to revalidate.

Jump: Executive What was tested Scores Per-finding detail Limitations

1. Executive summary

The scanner successfully discovered 25 artifacts (5 of each type) in a disposable temp sandbox and produced 325 pattern-scan findings. Unit tests: 41 passed (1.17s). DAST: skipped — adb not on PATH; device not available — DAST scaffold skipped (not forced). Reports were re-enriched with severity scores, CVSS-estimate, remediation, and revalidate guidance (same pipeline as the sandbox showcase) without re-downloading binaries.

Honest capability: solid inventory + metadata + constrained regex/metadata SAST. Not a full SAST/DAST product. Many hits (especially vague password strings) are noisy low/info leads. cvss_estimate is rule-based — not NVD CVSS for a CVE. CVE IDs are null (SCA backlog).

7. Per-finding detail (325)

Each card shows severity, severity_score, CWE/OWASP (and other taxonomy when present), cvss_estimate, How to fix (remediation), and How to revalidate. Sorted by severity then score. 325/325 findings have fix + revalidate text. Companion technical dump: report.html.

jar-secret-aws-access-key

\n#1\n
\n

AWS access key id-like string (AKIA…). (in config.properties; seen: api_key=AKIAIOSFODNN7EXAMPLE)

\n

<sandbox>\jar\edu-secrets-sample.jar

\n

Taxonomy & scoring

    \n
  • Severity: high
    • \n
  • severity_score: 9.2 / 10
    • \n
  • cvss_estimate: 8.6 AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:H/I:H/A:N
    rule-based estimate, not NVD CVSS for a CVE
    • \n
  • CVE: null (CVE IDs require component/version SCA; not invented here)
    • \n
  • CWE: CWE-798 (Hard-coded credentials)
    • \n
  • NIST: IA-5 (Authenticator management)
    • \n
  • OWASP: MASVS-AUTH-4 (No sensitive data in credentials)
    • \n
  • CAPEC: CAPEC-114 (Credential stuffing)
    • \n
  • CISA: NoSecrets (No cloud keys in JAR)
    • \n
  • NSA: AppSec (Protect cloud credentials (NSA))
    • \n
\n
How to fix (remediation)
Rotate the AWS access key immediately. Remove it from the app; use Cognito/STS or a backend that vends temporary credentials.
\n
How to revalidate
After rotation and rebuild, re-scan with --sast; secret-*/aws-access-key findings should be gone. Confirm key disabled in AWS IAM.
\n
\n

\nhigh\nseverity_score 8.8 / 10\n

dex-ssl-trust

\n#3\n
\n

Trust-all / disabled certificate validation heuristic. (seen: "()Ljavax/net/ssl/X509TrustManager;)

\n

<sandbox>\apk\AndroGoat.apk

\n

Taxonomy & scoring

    \n
  • Severity: high
    • \n
  • severity_score: 8.8 / 10
    • \n
  • cvss_estimate: 8.1 AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:H/I:H/A:N
    rule-based estimate, not NVD CVSS for a CVE
    • \n
  • CVE: null (CVE IDs require component/version SCA; not invented here)
    • \n
  • CWE: CWE-295 (Improper certificate validation)
    • \n
  • NIST: SC-8 (Transmission confidentiality), SC-23 (Session authenticity)
    • \n
  • OWASP: MASVS-NETWORK-2 (Certificate pinning / secure channel), M3 (Insecure communication)
    • \n
  • CAPEC: CAPEC-94 (Man in the middle)
    • \n
  • CISA: ValidateCerts (Validate TLS certificates; no trust-all)
    • \n
  • NSA: TLS (Proper certificate validation (NSA))
    • \n
\n
How to fix (remediation)
Remove custom TrustManager / HostnameVerifier implementations that accept all certificates. Use the system trust store; add certificate pinning only with a maintained pin set.
\n
How to revalidate
Rebuild and re-scan with --sast (and --dynamic if you use the Frida SSL check). Expect ssl-trust / trust-all rule_ids to clear.
\n

apk-debuggable

\n#22\n
\n

Application is debuggable; increases reverse-engineering risk.

\n

<sandbox>\apk\AndroGoat.apk

\n

Taxonomy & scoring

    \n
  • Severity: high
    • \n
  • severity_score: 8.4 / 10
    • \n
  • cvss_estimate: 7.5 AV:L/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:H/I:H/A:N
    rule-based estimate, not NVD CVSS for a CVE
    • \n
  • CVE: null (CVE IDs require component/version SCA; not invented here)
    • \n
  • CWE: CWE-489 (Active debug code)
    • \n
  • NIST: CM-7 (Least functionality), RA-5 (Vulnerability scanning)
    • \n
  • OWASP: MASVS-RESILIENCE-1 (App not debuggable in release), M9 (Reverse engineering (Mobile Top 10))
    • \n
  • CAPEC: CAPEC-169 (Footprinting)
    • \n
  • CISA: SecureBuild (Disable debug in production (CISA))
    • \n
  • NSA: MobileApp (Release builds: no debug (NSA))
    • \n
\n
How to fix (remediation)
Set android:debuggable=false for release builds (default when using a release build type). Do not ship debug APKs to production.
\n
How to revalidate
Build a release APK and re-run --sast; apk-debuggable should be absent when the manifest flag is false/unset for release.
\n
\n

\n#45\n
\n

Cleartext HTTP URL; prefer HTTPS. (seen: http://demo.testfire.net)

\n

<sandbox>\apk\AndroGoat.apk

\n

Taxonomy & scoring

    \n
  • Severity: medium
    • \n
  • severity_score: 5.5 / 10
    • \n
  • cvss_estimate: 7.4 AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:H/I:N/A:N
    rule-based estimate, not NVD CVSS for a CVE
    • \n
  • CVE: null (CVE IDs require component/version SCA; not invented here)
    • \n
  • CWE: CWE-319 (Cleartext transmission of sensitive data)
    • \n
  • NIST: SC-8 (Transmission confidentiality and integrity)
    • \n
  • OWASP: MASVS-NETWORK-1 (Secure data in transit (e.g. TLS)), M3 (Insecure communication (Mobile Top 10))
    • \n
  • CAPEC: CAPEC-94 (Man in the middle)
    • \n
  • CISA: EncryptTransit (Encrypt data in transit (CISA secure our world))
    • \n
  • NSA: Encrypt (Use TLS for all network traffic (NSA guidance))
    • \n
\n
How to fix (remediation)
Use HTTPS/TLS for all sensitive traffic. Replace http:// endpoints with https:// and enforce TLS in the network stack.
\n
How to revalidate
Rebuild and re-scan with --sast. Expect dex-/jar-/ipa-cleartext hits for those URLs to disappear (schema/example URLs may still match).

sdk-exposed-credential

\n#126\n
\n

Secret pattern in SDK file. File: config.properties

\n

<sandbox>\kits\training-mobile-sdk\config.properties

\n

Taxonomy & scoring

    \n
  • Severity: medium
    • \n
  • severity_score: 5.5 / 10
    • \n
  • cvss_estimate: 7.5 AV:L/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:H/I:N/A:N
    rule-based estimate, not NVD CVSS for a CVE
    • \n
  • CVE: null (CVE IDs require component/version SCA; not invented here)
    • \n
  • CWE: CWE-798 (Hard-coded credentials), CWE-312 (Cleartext storage of sensitive information)
    • \n
  • NIST: IA-5 (Authenticator management), SC-28 (Protection of information at rest)
    • \n
  • OWASP: MASVS-AUTH-4 (No sensitive data in credentials), M2 (Insecure data storage)
    • \n
  • CAPEC: CAPEC-114 (Credential stuffing), CAPEC-37 (Retrieve embedded sensitive data)
    • \n
  • CISA: NoSecrets (No credentials in config or source (CISA))
    • \n
  • NSA: SupplyChain (No credentials in SDK (NSA))
    • \n
\n
How to fix (remediation)
Remove credentials from SDK configs and source. Use environment injection or a secrets manager in consuming apps.
\n
How to revalidate
Re-scan with --sast; sdk-exposed-credential should clear when credential patterns are gone from configs.

  • CISA: EncryptAtRest (Encrypt sensitive data at rest)
    • \n
  • NSA: MobileApp (Encrypt app storage (NSA mobile))

8. Limitations / caveats

  • Pattern-scan / metadata heuristics only — not confirmed vulnerability triage.
  • Vague password/secret/apikey tokens are low/info noise by design.
  • No decompilation, call-graph, or API-misuse proof.
  • DEX strings via ZIP scan (androguard off); same constrained rules.
  • DAST not exercised (no adb/device).
  • cvss_estimate is rule-based — not NVD CVSS for a CVE; cve is null (SCA backlog).
  • One JAR (edu-secrets-sample.jar) was built locally for SAST coverage; others from Maven Central.
  • Native .so samples extracted from public training APKs (not standalone upstream .so releases).
  • Binaries purged from temp sandbox after report copy; not stored in git. Re-enrichment used existing report.json only.

9. How to open this report

Double-click manager_report.html in Explorer, or open via file:///…/docs/results/capability_run_20261014/manager_report.html. This page is self-contained (inline CSS + data) and works without a web server. Companion CLI dumps: report.json, report.html, report.csv, report.txt, summary.json.
Generated for capability demonstration. Findings re-enriched without re-scanning binaries. No exploit steps included.
